### Page 2

2【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
結論-缺料將加劇，把握上游和載板體系
1.PCB上游:供需吃緊+包產能+漲價，高階材料良率是考驗
• 銅箔:HVLP4銅箔月需求維持在2500~3000噸預估，生產難度較高，良率不穩導致供應吃緊，26H2~27年明
確吃緊。
• 鑽針:高階PCB大廠產值/產能跳增，高階鑽針報價翻升，產品組合推升單價和毛利率表現。
• 玻布:LowDK 2代布和T-Glass為主要缺貨來源，並帶動供應鏈積極漲價。
• 石英布:LPX Rack將使石英布呈現大幅供不應求，2027年潛在供需缺口約60%以上。
2. IC載板:2026Y缺料導致產能不足，2027~28年缺口將進一步擴大
• 主流AI晶片面積使用攀升，導致產能供不應求，福邦投顧預估2027~2028年缺口將進一步擴大至26%/46%。
• 歐美大客戶搶占主要ABF產能，ASIC體系面臨搶料+搶產能，相關報價可望優於傳統大客戶。
3. PCB中游:高階需求吃緊，海外產能優先，新供應體系躍升一線，傳統大廠緊追在後

• 需求吃緊:高階產能2026~2027見吃緊，供應鏈產能有限。
• 海外產能優先:定穎、金像電海外產能準備速度優於同業，獲得ASIC/GPU大單。
4. 建議首選關注個股:8046南電、3037欣興、1303南亞、南亞1802台玻、8021尖點、1815
富喬、2383 台光電、2368 金像電、3715 定穎、4958臻鼎

### Page 4

4【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
核心:政策主導AI為基本方針
大而美法案(One Big Beautiful Bill Act) AI行動計劃(America's AI Action Plan)
內容 1. 下調聯邦所得稅:從35%下調至21%，
減輕企業稅賦負擔。
2. 恢復2017年減稅與就業法案項目中
允許企業當年資本支出可以全額抵
減稅賦(折舊扣除)，有利重資本支出
廠商加快投資回收時間，受惠廠商
如CSP大廠。
3. 國內R&D全額費用化: 為了獎勵企業
在美國進行生產研發，符合條件的
R&D費用可以於當年度全面費用化，
受惠廠商如Open AI、Anthropic等。
1. 加速AI創新:確保美國擁有全球最強大
的AI系統，放鬆相關管制與障礙，並
鼓勵開源等。
2. 增加AI基礎設施建置:提升美國AI算力
基礎設施硬體基礎，以滿足AI推動經
濟和安全的需求；加快關鍵項目的審核，
加快建設資料中心、晶圓代工廠、電力
基礎設施，掃除一切AI基礎設施擴充
的障礙。
3. 維護國際外交與AI安全:在全球AI競爭

中掌握規則制定權，輸出美國AI技術，
同時阻止對手利用美國創新超越美國，
嚴防高階算力流向競爭國家，向盟友出
口美國AI算力技術等，有利主權
AI(Sovereign AI)的發展。。
• AI 成為美國政策上的基本方針，OBBBA法案刺激企業加大投資AI算力，AAAP計畫掃除建
置AI算力的瓶頸和主權AI方針的確立。
表1:OBBA法案和AI行動計畫
資料來源：福邦投顧整理

◼過去:依靠消費性電子起家的臻鼎、欣興、華通，依靠手機板的技術升級，奠定其全球一線板廠的
地位，過往營收排名No.1/No.2/No.5~6。
◼現在:AI成為PCB的主流需求，勝宏(營收排名No.14)模式的成功，給予很多傳統二線板廠轉型的模
板，透過團隊建立、併購、客戶關係的維繫下，擠進AI供應鏈的一線地位，傳統靠消費性起家的一線
板廠在AI的趨勢中，由於要切入過往不擅長的厚板，暫時處於落後的位置。
◼未來:擁有海外產能的板廠，在取得訂單上握有關鍵優勢，金像電、精成科(LCS)、定穎、勝宏等陸
續接到大廠訂單分配，也是當前客戶優先審廠/量產的對象。

### Page 12

12【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
PCB鑽針需求呈現翻倍成長
• PCB用鑽針受到板材影響，在同樣板厚下，每針可鑽孔次數大幅縮水:
M6~M7時主針鑽孔壽命約600~800次→M8材料下可鑽400~500次→M9材料下僅剩下100~200次
，材料越高階，鑽針使用量會是過往的6倍以上。
• 當前主流GPU用PCB約5mm厚度，隨著AI用PCB板厚的提升，每增加1mm，製造上可能會採
用增加一根預鑽針，採用預鑽針預鑽至特定深度，後續用主針完成加工。
• AI用的鑽針，長度/直徑比超過30規格的鑽針，報價上會採用一個方案一個價格，因為
加工精度要求提高，難度變大，目前AI用的鍍膜鑽針報價較傳統白針多20~30%，若是這
種報價方式，特別是板厚超過7mm，產品售價將會多40~50%；未來約在2027~2028年時，
7.5~8.5mm的鑽針將成為主流，報價將會是一般白針的6倍以上報價。
• AI用的鑽針，根據產業訪查，產品毛利率約40～50%。
過去 目前 未來
對應材質 M6~M7 M8 M9

主針壽命 1000/600~800次 400~500次 100~200次(LK2)/50~100次(Q布)
GPU Server ASIC Server 交換機

### Page 13

OpenClaw:加速從LLM往AI Agent 發展
◼ OpenClaw:開放式AI助理項目，可以根據使用者指令在應用程式和線上服務之間自行處
理複雜任務，並被設計為可以代替使用者執行任務的AI軟體，並非過往的聊天機器人。
◼ OpenAI推出GPT5.4，此次更新提供Thinking和Pro兩種模型； Pro主要適用於需要進行
複雜任務的使用者，Thinking模型可以在產出答案前提出思考計畫，使用者可以從中調
整任務方向→強化Agent能力，可以支持智慧裝置執行複雜的工作流程。
◼ NVIDIA將推出類似OpenClaw的開放式平台-NemoClaw。
資料來源:產業調研、福邦投顧整理

### Page 25

25【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
大廠對於CPU的關注度提昇
資料來源:產業調研、福邦投顧整理預估
表17：主流大廠對於Agentic AI的想法
看法
Intel • 在Agentic AI 浪潮中，傳統 CPU 重回重要位置;公司強調隨著 AI 代理（agent）工作負
載增多，CPU 扮演了協調各種運算的核心角色。
• Intel 也坦承先前低估了需求：Intel「錯估了」數據中心 CPU 的增長速度，正將更多晶
圓產能從客戶端 (PC) 轉向伺服器用處理器。這些訊息顯示，Intel 正積極將資源集中
在伺服器 CPU，並強調其 x86 生態系統與 GPU 夥伴（如與 NVIDIA 合作的 CPU+GPU
解決方案）將支持未來 AI 工作負載。
AMD • 目前伺服器級 CPU 的需求「遠遠超出」預期。推理工作負載增加明顯拉動 CPU 使用
量增長，不僅標準 CPU（如 EPYC）需求強勁，而且客戶也要求針對特定 AI 工作負
載做客製化設計。
• Agentic AI 流程中，多數「派生工作」（由智能代理產生的副任務）會落在傳統

CPU 上處理，因此 EPYC CPU 需求正「激增」，並預期伺服器端的 CPU 市場會以兩
位數速度成長。
• AMD 認為 Agentic AI 使它的 CPU 產品戰略得到驗證：不只 GPU，CPU 也是驅動 AI
效能和擴展性的關鍵。
NVIDIA • 隨著 AI 流量從訓練階段轉向部署和推理階段，公司會將 Grace/Vera CPU 與 GPU
搭配，服務 Agentic 工作負載
• 未來推理繁重的部署可能會更接近 1:1 的 CPU:GPU 比例，甚至有時可完全以 CPU
獨自處理。公司最近宣布與 Meta 達成協議，大規模部署 NVIDIA 的 Grace/Vera
CPU 作為獨立平台，顯示其對 CPU 解決方案的積極推廣。
資料來源:各公司、福邦投顧整理

### Page 26

26【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Agentic AI的瓶頸主要來自CPU
◼ 根據論文《A CPU-Centric Perspective on Agentic AI》一文指出，可以看出執行時間中
CPU延遲占了大比例（紅圈），最高可達到90%以上；吞吐量瓶頸除了來自GPU，亦會
來自CPU;能耗方面，大批次處理中(Batch size提升至128)，CPU平行處理效率低於GPU
，佔到總能耗超過44%，延遲會從2.8秒提升至6秒以上，超過2倍。
資料來源: 《A CPU-Centric Perspective on Agentic AI》 、福邦投顧整理
說明:Haystack RAG（問答檢索）/Toolformer（數學運算與 API 呼叫）/ChemCrow（化學文獻檢索）
Langchain（網路搜尋與摘要）/SWE-Agent（程式碼與 Bash/Python 執行)
圖9:：Agent下，CPU佔據了大部分的延遲

### Page 27

27【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPU朝向多核發展→推升載板面積
◼ Agent執行:API處理、資料解析、腳本執行，90%由CPU完成，為了降低延遲，CPU往多
核心發展是趨勢。
◼ 記憶體與上下文切換:CPU需要頻繁在不同Agent狀態做切換，多核心架構能讓每個
Agent有獨立的處理單元，降低上下文切換帶來的效率損耗。
◼ 沙盒隔離/安全:Agent 需要大量獨立的一般伺服器建立Sandbox來執行，使用量與使用
者和使用量正相關，需要更多核心數、高頻寬。
資料來源:Intel、AMD、福邦投顧整理預估

34【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
交換機板:主流AI層數較低，傳統乙太網多層為主
◼ 資料中心需要承載流量大+高速傳輸，在此需求下，高多層板成為此需求的解決方案，
其優點在於電子元件間連線縮短下，訊號傳輸速度提高，而且也方遍佈線。
◼乙太網交換機為各家CSP+主權主要Scale out的主流，800G在層數規格和報價上均較
400G有明顯成長。
◼根據板廠和材料廠的訪查，大廠配合的材料商:Amazon(斗山)、Google(松下)、Meta(台
燿/台光電)、微軟(台燿/台光電)、Nvidia(台光電/生益?)，整體800G的供應仍以斗山>台
燿>松下/台光電。

2408.TT 南亞科

[法說會更新]   
   公司簡介 
南亞科成立於 1995 年，為 DRAM 之研發、設計、製造與銷售商，於美國、歐洲、日
本、中國設立海外行銷據點，全球市占率約 1%，為台灣第一大 DRAM 製造商。營收
佔比方面，DDR3 約 20%、DDR4 約 70%、DDR5 約 10%。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
強力買進 750 
【 前 次 投 資 建 議 】強 力 買 進，565 
【大盤指數/股價】45355 / 435.5

➢ 2Q26 持續受惠合約價上漲，毛利率優於預期 
2Q26 營收 825.49 億元，QoQ+68.17%，YoY+684.23%，營收成長動
能主要仍來自於供需吃緊帶動 ASP 漲幅季增逾 60%，位元出貨量約略
與 1Q26 持平所致 。2Q26 毛利率 79.49%， 營業費用因研發與員工分紅
略 高 於 預 期 ， 惟 新 台 幣 貶 值 挹 注 匯 兌 利 益 ，EPS 為 14.66 元 ，
QoQ+92.6%。 
➢ 3Q26 ASP 漲幅可望逾 50%，營收獲利大幅增長 
展望 3Q26，預期 ASP 漲幅可望優於原廠漲幅，預估季增逾 50%，同
時，隨著1b 良率提升，3Q26 位元出貨量有望較 2Q26 微幅增加，整體
而言 ， 預估3Q26 營收約 1,255.56 億元 ，QoQ+52.1%，YoY+568.6%，
毛利率約 86.57%，EPS 為 24.15 元，QoQ+66.03%，YoY+5,230.47%。  
➢ 2H26 DDR4 16G 貢獻營收，2027 年新廠開出，營運持續升溫 
展望 2026 年，考量 3Q26 ASP 有望大幅優於投研部先前預期，且上漲
動能將至少延續至 2027 年，此外，位元出貨量預估約年增 20%，上調
2026 年營收至約 3,980 億元 ，YoY+497.74%， 毛利率83.24%，EPS 為
74.71 元，YoY+3,747%。 
展望 2027 年，新廠機台預估 2Q27 開始投片，最快將於 4Q27 開始貢
獻營收，屆時月產能預計將較 2026 年新增 2 萬片。需求端方面，單機
DRAM 搭載量的增加將使得總需求量持續成長，整體產業仍將處於供不
應求市況當中 ， 預期ASP 仍有望小幅度上漲 。 預估2027 年營收約 6,465
億元 ，YoY+62.43%， 毛利率87.49%，EPS 為 126.45 元，YoY+71.49%。  
➢ 2H26 DDR4 16G 貢獻營收，2027 年新廠開出，營運持續升溫 
評價方面，考量先前愷俠、SANDISK、SOLIDIGM 等入股南亞科，客戶
結構出現改變且訂單需求強勁，將有助擺脫過往傳統景氣循環影響，且
預估 2027 年 DDR5 佔比將逐步提升。 看好隨著AI 發展 ， 記憶體已經上
升至重要戰略資源，同時短期供給端難以出現大量新產能，南亞科是少
數於 2027 年可以擴產並貢獻營收的記憶體廠，再加上公司正與多個客
戶進行客製化 DRAM 的合作 ， 有助提升長線獲利能見度 ， 故投研部改以
本益比進行評價，維持強力買進建議，目標價 750 元(2026F PE 10X)。

2408 南亞科 加權指數

個股報告

公司簡介 
南亞科成立於 1995 年，為 DRAM(動態隨機存取記憶體)之研發、設計、製造與銷售
商，於美國、歐洲、日本、中國設立海外行銷據點，全球市占率約1%，為全球第五大
DRAM 製造商、台灣第一大 DRAM 製造商，1Q26 競爭同業市占分別為 SK 海力士
(000660 KS) 28.8%、三星(005930 KS) 38.5%、美光(MU US)22.4%；DRAM 上游的
生產材料為矽晶圓，南亞科主要向台勝科(3532)購買；DRAM 下游的封裝與測試則由
福懋科(8131)進行，南亞科主要股東為台塑集團的南亞塑膠，營收佔比方面， DDR3
約 20%、DDR4 約 70%、DDR5 約 10%。應用別方面，Server+PC 約 20%~25%，
eSSD 約 10%，車用與工業約 10%，消費性產品&其他約 55%。 
2Q26 持續受惠合約價上漲，毛利率優於預期 
2Q26 營收 825.49 億元，QoQ+68.17%，YoY+684.23%，營收成長動能主要仍來自
於供需吃緊帶動 ASP 漲幅季增逾 60%，並且位元出貨量約略與 1Q26 持平所致。獲
利表現方面，2Q26 毛利率 79.49%，QoQ+11.62ppts，YoY+100.06ppts，優於預
期，主要仍受惠於 ASP 上漲所帶動、1b 製程良率提升以及 DDR4 8Gb 營收占比持續
提升優化產品組合所致。營業費用雖然因研發費用與員工分紅等支出略高於預期，惟
新台幣貶值挹注匯兌利益，2Q26 稅後淨利為 501.92 億元，QoQ+92.6%，EPS 為
14.66 元。產品組合方面，考量當前 DDR4/LPDDR4 供給極為有限，公司仍將出貨重
心擺在 DDR4/LPDDR4，故出貨占比約 7 成。應用領域方面，包含車用、工業及部分
通訊的廣義消費型電子產品佔比超過 60%，AI 基礎設施與伺服器相關的營收佔比則
超過 20%。 
3Q26 ASP 漲幅可望逾 50%，營收獲利大幅增長 
從整體 DRAM 市場來看，市場的主要變化仍在於 AI 應用帶動的結構性轉變正在減緩
過去記憶體市場明顯的週期性波動，需求端正經歷由 B2C 向 B2B 轉移的現象。生成
式 AI 的硬體架構推升了 GPU、CPU、TPU 及 ASIC 對 HBM、LPDDR5 及 DDR5 元
件的使用量。同時，AI 基礎設施也增加了企業級 SSD、智慧網卡與 BMC 等採用較大
容量與高效能 DRAM 的比例。在供給端方面，AI 伺服器相關記憶體產能的增加，排
擠了部分智慧型手機 、 個人電腦及一般消費性電子的記憶體供給額度 。 非AI 應用領域
在產能受限的情況下，市場供應轉向偏好高階產品型號，導致終端市場出現定價結構
的調整。整體而言，短期內 B2B 的強勁需求與供給瓶頸仍難以改變，預期 3Q26 原廠
DRAM 報價漲幅可望季增逾 30%。 
展望 3Q26，預期營運動能將延續，獲利表現可望持續優化。在 ASP 方面，考量部分
客戶仍有換約需求，合約價具調漲空間，以及 DDR4 8Gb 為與原廠報價切齊，預估
3Q26 南亞科 ASP 漲幅可望優於原廠漲幅，投研部預估季增逾 50%，同時，隨著 1b
良率提升，預期 3Q26 位元出貨量有望較 2Q26 微幅增加，整體而言，預估 3Q26 營
收約 1,255.56 億元， QoQ+52.1% ， YoY+568.6% ，毛利率約 86.57% ，
QoQ+7.08ppts ， YoY+68.12ppts ， 稅 後 淨 利 948.55 億 元 ， QoQ+66.03% ，
YoY+5,230.47%，EPS 為 24.15 元。 
2H26 DDR4 16G 貢獻營收，2027 年新廠開出，營運持續升溫 
展望 2026 年，隨著原廠 DDR4 陸續 EOL，預期 2H26 DDR4 供需缺口將進一步放
大，尤其工控、網通等客戶仍停留在DDR4，並且以 16Gb DDR4 和 LPDDR4 需求尤
為強勁 。 南亞科目前持續優化產能提供DDR4 的需求 ， 並且維持原先1H26 送樣 16Gb 
DDR4，2H26 貢獻營收的計畫。綜上所述，考量 3Q26 ASP 有望大幅優於投研部先

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
前預期，且上漲動能將至少延續至 2027 年，此外 ，位元出貨量方面 ， 投研部預估2026
年位元出貨量約年增 20%，故上調 2026 年營收至約 3,980 億元，YoY+497.74%，
毛利率 83.24%，YoY+60.73ppts，除了受惠 ASP 上漲及良率提升外，全年折舊亦有
下滑加上 DDR4 16GB 開始貢獻營收優化產品組合，共同推升獲利表現，稅後淨利
2,544.4 億元，YoY+3747%，EPS 為 74.71 元。 
展望 2027 年，公司表示新廠機台將於 1Q27 移入，預估 2Q27 開始投片，最快將於
4Q27 開始貢獻營收，屆時月產能預計將較 2026 年新增 2 萬片，全年位元年成長率
逾 10%。需求端方面，edge AI 與 AI PC 的發展預期將成為改變 DRAM 搭載量的重
要成長動能，如支援地端推論的 AI PC 主記憶體需求有機會達 128GB，而雲端伺服器
單一 CPU 推論模組的記憶體配置需求亦達 1.5TB，硬體規格的升級將對記憶體市場總
量產生額外需求，故當智慧型手機或傳統個人電腦的總銷量增長放緩時， 單機DRAM
搭載量的增加將使得總需求量持續成長，整體產業仍將處於供不應求市況當中，預期
ASP 仍有望小幅度上漲。預估 2027 年營收約 6465 億元，YoY+62.43%，毛利率
87.49%，YoY+4.25ppts， 稅後淨利約4,363 億元 ，YoY+71.49%，EPS 為 126.45 元。 
DRAM 成為 AI 成長重要資源，長線獲利能見度提升，維持強力買進 
整體而言，隨著原廠陸續將產能轉往更高階的 HBM 及 DDR5，而需求面仍有工控、
網通以及消費性客戶採用 DDR4，供需緊張態勢將至少延續至 2027 年，乃至 2028
年，仍看好 DRAM 合約價將呈現上行趨勢。針對市場對於 2027 年或 2028 年同業新
產能開出可能導致供需失衡的疑慮 ， 若將伺服器供應商宣告的擴產規模換算為EUV 設
備的實質產能增幅，整體的產能擴張幅度仍處於業界常態範圍內，加上多數新建產能
已具備 LTA 的基礎 ， 因此推估市場在未來兩年不致出現供過於求 。 為應對中長期需求 ，
南亞科總投資額 160 億美元的新廠建置計畫按既定進度執行 ， 目標為打造每月4.5 萬
片的產能，新廠初期的製程規劃將優先採用現有良率穩定的 16 奈米產線設定，以確
保能即時生產 DDR4 或 DDR5 產品，同步為下一代 1C 製程的量產爭取技術調校時
間。投研部看好隨著 Agentic AI 需求增加，以及 AI 伺服器對於 eSSD 的用量提升，
將持續加劇 DRAM 供需緊張態勢；另中國記憶體供給增加方面，投研部預期其主要
受惠對象僅為中國境內 PC、 手機等受到伺服器需求排擠的消費性電子客戶 ， 而綜觀全
球需求，伺服器已經成為 DRAM 最大的需求來源，並且隨著 CPU 的使用量增加，將
進一步推升 DRAM 需求量，故投研部認為中國即便增加供給，仍對於整體供需影響
相對有限。 
在技術發展與新產品開發進度上，南亞科的 1c、1d、1e 製程節點以及 EUV 技術導入
計畫皆按既定時程推進。其中，1c 預計於 2026 年底至 1Q27 進入準備階段，並將逐
步進行良率學習與產品驗證。在產品線方面，公司維持產品組合的廣度，持續提供涵
蓋 DDR3、DDR4、LPDDR4 及 DDR5 與 LPDDR5 產品。目前公司具備 16Gb 產品的
量產能力，以供應伺服器 BMC、企業級 SSD 等應用的需求。同時，透過 2Q26 完成
的私募案，公司與包含企業級 SSD 及網通設備在內的國際客戶建立了至少三年的長
期合作與供應承諾，將成為支持公司產品線發展的基礎。 
評價方面，考量當前 DRAM 報價漲勢仍然強勁且優於預期，加上 2026 年折舊下滑、
良率增加帶動毛利率表現，預期獲利、營運動能將強於往年循環。此外，考量先前愷
俠、SANDISK、SOLIDIGM 等入股南亞科，為確保 eSSD 在 DDR4 的貨源充足，故
客戶結構出現改變且訂單需求強勁 ， 將有助擺脫過往傳統景氣循環影響 ， 且預估2027
年 DDR5 佔比將逐步提升。投研部看好隨著 AI 發展，記憶體已經上升至重要戰略資
源，同時考量短期供給端難以出現大量新產能，南亞科是少數於 2027 年可以擴產並

### Page 4

- 4 -

個股報告 
貢獻營收的記憶體廠，再加上公司正與多個客戶進行客製化 DRAM 的合作，其中亦
包含 WoW 產品，有助提升長線獲利能見度，故投研部改以本益比進行評價，維持強
力買進建議，目標價 750 元(2026F PE 10X)。

南亞科 (2408 TT)

2Q26 毛利率挑戰 80%大關

目標價上調至 548 元，潛在上漲空間+34% 
1. 4-6 月合併營收 825.49 億元，QoQ+68.2%，YoY+684.2%，優於預期。 
2. 美光 SCA 將趨動商業模式重大轉型，產業毛利率谷底料在 60%以上。 
3. 富邦估南亞科 FY26~28 單季毛利率區間約落在 62~85%之間。 
上修 2Q26 毛利率估值，單季 EPS 估 15.12 元 
南亞科累積 4-6 月合併營收 825.49 億元，QoQ+68.2%，YoY+684.2%，數字優於
富邦預期。TrendForce 統計 DRAM 均價 2Q26 上揚約 5-6 成，南亞科所處利基型
DRAM 漲幅略高且存在補漲效應。因此，我們預估南亞科 2Q26 銷售均價 QoQ 上
揚約 7 成、銷售量 QoQ 下滑低個位數，因此預估毛利率將升抵達 81.2%，較前季
提升 13.3 個百分點。簡言之，我們預估南亞科 2Q26 營業利益估約 629.21 億元，
歸屬母公司稅後淨利估約 521.82 億元，EPS 估約 15.12 元。

美光 SCA 揭示記憶體商業模式的重大轉型 
美光於 6 月 24 日法說指出目前已簽署 16 份策略客戶協議(SCAs)涵蓋了數據中心 、
消費性及汽車市場，旨在提供長期的供應保障與技術合作。SCA 多數期間為 5 年
(2026 至 2030 年)，採執行或賠償(take-or-pay)模式，要求客戶對特定採購量做
出具約束力的承諾 。據此， 美光預計將收到220 億美元的財務承諾 ； 其中包含 180 
億美元的現金存款(美光可自由支用)，在 SCA 順利執行的情況下，將在 2030 年到
期前歸還客戶。在財務機制上，多數SCA 設有價格上限與保底價格；即使在保底價
格之下 ， 美光的毛利率仍將遠高於過去任何週期的峰值(富邦推測>60%)。 管理層指
出，SCAs 目前規模已涵蓋美光約 20% 的 DRAM 銷量與 33%的 NAND 銷量，預
期未來公司一半以上的營收將納入此類型框架。簡言之，SCA 模式顯著增強了記憶
體廠營運的可預測性與穩定性，使業者能更有效地應對 AI 驅動的長期需求。

2H26 價格小幅向上，全年 EPS 估逾 60 元 
展望下半年，集邦預估 3Q26 Consumer DRAM 均價 QoQ 上漲約 28~40%、其他
傳統 DRAM 漲幅約 10~20%；4Q26 整體 DRAM 漲幅約+3~8%。南亞科在利基
DRAM 出貨仍為主流 ，因此預料 3Q26 均價將季增約 25~27%、 銷售量季減約3%，
合併營收估約 1,006.43 億元 ，QoQ+21.9%，YoY+435.9%； 毛利率估升至85.2%，
EPS 估約 19.34 元。至於 4Q26，價格上揚估 2~5%，料大致抵消銷售量下滑，合
併營收、毛利率大致持平或微減，EPS 估約 18.87 元，累積全年 EPS 約 60.89 元。

富邦不同之處 
富邦修正南亞科最新之 2026-27 年 EPS 預估值分別為 60.89、78.75 元，相較目前
CMoney 同業平均預估值分別+39%、+46%，數字相對樂觀。

評價與風險 
美光 6 月 24 日法說訊息指出 SCA 將限制未來產品價格天花板與下限 ， 推測未來產
業毛利率將落在 60~89%之間。我們看好 SCA 商業模式有助產業基本面維持平穩
發展，預估南亞科 FY26-28EPS 之波動將小於歷史區間，並上修獲利預估值、維持
買進的投資評等，目標價上調至 548 元(約 FY26 的 9.0PER 或 FY4.2XPBR)。南亞
科的股價風險來自(1)高階伺服器產品或私募股東合作進度不如預期、(2)AI 產業展
望出現意外的結構修正。

伺服器佈局逐步擴大，入列高通 HBC 協力廠 
南亞科正逐步擴張在伺服器與 AI 運算的記憶體產品線 ，2026 年將是擴大放量元年 。
首先，泰林廠 CY27 量產初期每月 2 萬片，最大產能可擴充至每月 4.5 萬片，其製
程將由 1B 向 1D 逐步過渡，專注的產品將由 DDR5 標準品向 RDIMM(標準品)、
UltraWIO(Ultra Wide I/O ，非標準品 )升級。南亞科官網 顯示 DDR4/5 RDIMM 
8/16/32GB 已可供應；64/128GB 產品則還在開發之中。轉投資福懋科正在擴充
DDR5 R-DIMM 模組的生產線，2H26 正處於密集移入機台與試產之中；因此我們
預期 RDIMM 模組將在 2027 年步入更全面的大量生產。其次，南亞科入列高通
HBC(High-Bandwidth Compute)的記憶體協力廠 ， 推測南亞科將負責LPDDR5 的
TSV 堆疊製程，以滿足高通 HBC 的近記憶體(Near-Memory)運算架構所需。富邦
推測該產品有機會成為南亞科泰林新廠的伺服器高階產品的出海口之一，確認南亞
科在 AI 時代記憶體產業的獨特戰略位置。

SCA 定義中期毛利率底部，南亞科區間估約 62~85% 
代理式 AI 時代重塑記憶體產業供需格局已是目前市場共識，我們預期二大事件將
明確定義出 2028 年以前的產業走勢。首先，三大原廠新增產能將自 2027 年中開
始挹注銷售，並在 2028 年成為後續價格反轉的主要力量。其次，美光SCA 條款將
使產業劇烈波動的景氣循環 ， 引導走向營收和獲利更平穩 、 更可預測的軌跡。 因此 ，
我們預測記憶體價格 2H27 開始出現有意義的修正，並持續到 1H28，累積波段跌
幅估約 40-50%；換算產品毛利率約落在 60~65%。在上述框架下，富邦預估南亞
科 2027、28 年 EPS 分別為 78.75、65.11 元，波段毛利率高點約為 85%、低點約
為 62%。

Fubon's Rating System 
Rating Definition 
Buy Expected absolute return to be over 15% within the next 6 months  
Neutral  Expected absolute return to be level within the next 6 months  
Sell  Expected absolute return to be over negative 15% within the next 6 months 
Not Rated (NR)  Pursuant to Fubon acting in deals involving the company or there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an investment rating  
Under Review Fubon is in the process of determining an investment rating and will assign a rating within the 3- to 6-month horizon

Sector Rating Definition 
Overweight Sector expected to outperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months 
Market Weight  Sector expected to perform in line with the broader market averages within the next 6-12 months 
Underweight  Sector expected to underperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months.

### Page 1

Summary: EMC announced its June revenue of TWD17.7bn (up 14% MoM and 121% 
YoY), which makes its 2Q sales of TWD47.3bn (up 43% QoQ and 110% YoY) and 
achieves 112% of our 2Q26 revenue forecast (TWD42.2bn; up 28% QoQ and 88% YoY) 
and 114% of Bloomberg consensus (TWD41.5bn). The actual results were higher than 
market expectations of 15-20% QoQ growth, while the better-than-expected price hike 
effect contributed around two-thirds of QoQ growth magnitude (ie.28% QoQ), with the 
remainder driven by an improved revenue mix (lower contribution from handset and 
automotive). We believe c.28% price hike should already factor in most of the cost 
increases from raw material since 2H25 to 1Q26. However, management doesn’t rule out 
the possibility of further raising prices in 2H26 given the on-going price increase in raw 
materials from 2Q26. We see EMC as the leader in CCL market and expect it to continue 
benefitting from ASP increases (at least 30% every generation) due to the high speed 
transmission requirements driven by AI mega trend. As such, we remain positive on EMC, 
with a Buy (1) on the name. Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected AI server demand.

EMC: monthly revenue trend and YoY growth

Source: Company

What’s new:  
EMC announced its June revenue of TWD17.7bn (up 14% MoM and 121%YoY), which 
makes its 2Q sales of TWD47.3bn (up 43% QoQ and 110% YoY) and achieves 112% of 
our 2Q26 revenue forecast (TWD42.2bn; up 28% QoQ and 88% YoY) and 114% of the 
Bloomberg consensus (TWD41.5bn). The actual results were higher than market 
expectations of 15-20% QoQ growth, while the better-than-expected price hike effect 
contributed around two-thirds of QoQ growth magnitude (ie.28% QoQ), with the remainder 
driven by an improved revenue mix (lower contribution from handset and automotive). 
Although EMC raised its prices to pass through the additional material costs (eg. copper, 
glass fibre, etc) at a slightly lower pace, which weighed on gross margin in 1Q26, we 
believe the c.30% price hike in 2Q26 should already cover most of cost inflation from 2H25 
to 1Q26.

Looking ahead, management doesn’t rule out the possibility of further price hikes as some 
raw material costs rise again (eg. low dk and low dk2 glass fibre, copper foil, etc). 
However, the magnitude (if any) would be lower than that in 2Q26. We expect EMC to 
continue acting as the global CCL leader and to benefit from continued ASP increases due 
to spec upgrade trend. Based on our understanding, Nvidia would migrate to M8+ or M9Q 
from Rubin and to M9, M9Q, M10, or PTFE from Rubin Ultra. As for the ASIC camp,  it 
would migrate to M9 or M9Q starting next year from the current M8+.

While EMC already has a higher revenue mix from high end CCL grades (eg. 70% in 
2026E) and key handset clients, we expect the room for EMC to further optimize product 
mix to be smaller than that of peers. As such, the new capacity will have to wait until 4Q26 
from EMC’s Guanyin plant.

What we recommend:  
We have a Buy (1) rating on EMC with a 12M TP of TWD6,022, based on a target PER of 
48x applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected 
AI server demand.

Summary: Innodisk reported another record-high monthly revenue for June 2026 of 
TWD8,208m (+8.5% MoM and +696.3% YoY; +530.9% YoY for 6M26) on 6 July. Its 2Q26 
revenue of TWD22,443m accounted for 153.0% and 134.5% of our and the Bloomberg 
consensus estimates of 2Q26 revenue. We attribute the solid revenue growth to the flash 
price hike.

We have a Buy (1) rating on the stock with a 12-month TP of TWD2,385, based on a target 
PER of 10x, applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. For more information on this 
company, please refer to our flash, Record-high monthly revenue boosted by Flash price 
hike, on 5 June 2026.

What’s the impact 
• Record-high monthly revenue boosted by flash price hike. Innodisk reported a 
record-high monthly revenue in June 2026 of TWD8,208m (+8.5% MoM and +696.3% 
YoY; +530.9% YoY for 6M26) and its 2Q26 revenue of TWD22,443m (+70.3% QoQ and 
+641.1% YoY), also a record-high, accounted for 153.0% and 134.5% of our 
(TWD14,671m, +11.3% QoQ and +384.4% YoY) and the Bloomberg consensus 
forecasts of 2Q26 revenue, respectively. We attribute the solid revenue growth to the 
flash price hike.

Innodisk: consolidated monthly sales

Source: Company

• Much stronger demand over supply. Innodisk expects its revenue momentum to 
continue into 2H26. The company had expected its gross margin to further improve in 
2Q26, thanks to the flash price hike. Due to the strong visibility, the company is currently 
selective with orders and is focusing on value creation across applications, clients and 
products. Its AI-related revenue may exceed 30% in 2026, up from 25% in 2025, 
according to management.

Document 081: 260706_daiwa_Nanya Technology

Source PDF: all report pdf/260706_daiwa_Nanya Technology.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-07-06
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Nanya announced its June revenue after market hours on 3 
July. It posted robust revenue growth in 2Q26 (68% QoQ, 684% YoY) led 
by DRAM price hikes despite limited bit shipments. With the DDR4 
premium over DDR5 maintained, we expect major players' supply 
reductions to prolong the supply shortage and price uplift into 2027. Nanya 
will hold its 2Q26 conference call on 10 July. With a positive market outlook 
and earnings forecasts, we expect the announcement of an enhanced 
shareholder return plan given the visibility of mid- to long-term demand.

What’s the impact: 2Q26 revenue exceeds consensus. Nanya posted 
revenue of TWD82.5bn (68% QoQ, 684% YoY) in 2Q26, beating the 
Bloomberg consensus. We also assume its operating profit of TWD49.0bn 
(101% QoQ, turned positive YoY) was higher than consensus. While we 
assume limited bit shipments (-3% QoQ) for 2Q26, given a reduced 
inventory level, we expect an earnings surprise led by a significant increase 
in DRAM prices of 68% QoQ, driven by sustained strong demand. We 
expect further earnings improvement in 3Q26, fuelled by continued DRAM 
price momentum.

Further earnings upside in 2H26E, with DDR4 price momentum likely 
to extend into 2027. Per Trendforce’s report on 30 June, consumer DRAM 
remained in shortage through June, extending the price uptrend. Notably, 
not only DDR4 but DDR3 and DDR2 have also seen significant price hikes. 
With major DRAM players focusing on HBM and server DRAM, upward 
pressure on legacy product pricing continues to build. We also expect PC 
DRAM prices to rise by 20% QoQ in 3Q26. As enterprise SSD demand 
surges due to rising inference AI demand, we assume strong buffer DRAM 
demand to be sustained into 2027 and beyond, based on long-term 
agreements (LTA). Backed by a solid earnings outlook, we expect Nanya to 
sustain or raise its payout ratio of 50% over 2027-28, despite management 
having guided for a potential cut due to heavy capex in April. We raise our 
2026–2028 EPS by 37–91% reflecting the DRAM price hikes.

What we recommend: We reiterate our Buy (1) rating and raise our 12-
month TP to TWD650 (from TWD292), based on a target PER of 9.2x (from 
6.8x) on our 2026-27E EPS (from 2026E EPS). Our new multiple applies a 
25% premium to the average PER of major DRAM peers, reflecting upside 
from the buffer DRAM LTA secured via private placement with global 
customers on 25 March. Downside risk: weaker-than-expected commodity 
DRAM demand due to delays in data centre build-out.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E EPS are 27-41% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely as we are more positive about the DDR4 price outlook.

6 July 2026

Solid 2Q26 revenue: intact earnings momentum

➢ 2Q26 revenue exceeds Bloomberg consensus on DRAM price hikes 
➢ Further earnings upside in 2H26E; DDR4 price momentum until 2027E 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; raising 12-month TP to TWD650

Company profile 
Nanya manufactures dynamic random access memory (DRAM). Focusing on consumer DRAM market, 
Nanya had developed its technology through cooperation with other advanced suppliers such as Micron. 
Recently, as the fourth largest player in the global DRAM market, Nanya plans to apply its own process 
(1anm, 1bnm) developed to manufacture DRAM, targeting the mobile and server DRAM market. Nanya 
currently has wafer capacity of c.70k units, of which the 20nm-process accounts for 80% and is centred 
in Taiwan.

Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
June  Sales Fell 10% MoM, Rose
73% YoY
In this report, we focus on Chroma's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for share price movement.
Details:
• June revenue was NT$4,114mn (-10% MoM/+73% YoY).
• 2Q26 revenue reached NT$13,529mn (+14% QoQ/+110% YoY), exceeding
MSe of NT$12,575mn (+6% QoQ/+95% YoY) by 8%  and consensus of NT
$12,704mn (+7% QoQ/+97% YoY) by 6%.
Our view:
• We expect Chroma to deliver strong revenue growth in 2026, driven by the

power testing (AI power product capacity expansions, emerging HVDC
migration, and ESS upturn) and semi & photonics (new SL T capex cycle,
metrology for advanced packaging and photonics testing for transceivers/
CPO) businesses  amid AI deployment.
• Moreover, we expect its FT handlers and burn-in ovens to start contributing
in the coming years, as it is qualifying with potential customers now.
• Stay OW.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 50x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of
~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 50x is justified by the 53% operating profit CAGR we project for

2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand
n Margin improvement via better product mix
n Better opex control
Risks to Downside
n Macro slowdown
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower progress in the advanced packaging capacity expansion
n Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand
n Margin contraction arising from lack of revenue scale and increasing opex

Connecting Dots in Tech    Supply

TSMC has reportedly    pulled forward CoPoS to address Intel
EMIB-T competition. MediaTek    TPU volumes have upside after
securing Japan T-Glass. Taiwan's CPO    supply chain development
remains on track; Glassbridge seems remote    at this stage.
What’s new?    Investors are asking how we reconcile views and research from tech
supply chain analysts, which may sometimes look inconsistent. Last     year’s most

popular example was upstream CoWoS capacity-implied     chips vs. AI server rack
consumption (    link    ). We try to connect the dots here.
1. T-Glass shortage vs. TPU volume upside: MediaTek gets      more T-Glass supply
from Japan for its 3nm Google TPU project   .    In    MediaTek: Upside in both cloud and
edge chip business; OW (23      Jun 2026)    , we noted that MediaTek can assist Google
(covered by Brian Nowak)     in sourcing more T-Glass from the Japanese fiber material

vendor to     secure supply. Hence, we see upside to our shipment and revenue
assumptions for 3nm ZebraFish TPU – we now forecast 3mn units in     2027. The
question here is – how can MediaTek source even more     T-Glass supply?
Our Japan manufacturing analyst, Yoshihito Hasegawa, believes that     Nitto Boseki,
which he covers, is the leading T-glass supplier for     Google's TPU. According to our
hardware analyst, Howard Kao, Zhen     Ding is likely MediaTek’s key second source for

3nm TPU substrates     alongside Unimicron. However, Howard believes Zhen Ding's
primary     value drivers are its exposure to China AI chip ABF substrates,     optical
transceiver module PCBs, and AI server PCBs rather than TPUs     (    Zhen Ding: Strong
preliminary May profits [22 Jun 2026])    . See Howard’s analysis of CoWoS capacity-
implied substrate demand     vs. ABF substrate supply gap (    Exhibit 3     ).
2. TSMC CoPoS pulled forward?    According to    news reports    , TSMC’s CoPoS will

collaborate with Ibiden (covered by Shoji Sato)     for substrates, and with Innolux
(covered by Derrick Yang) focusing     on TGV (Through Glass Vias) for Nvidia’s
Feynman GPU. TSMC guided     that CoPoS mass production should be from
2028/2029. Glass core     substrates are critical to large reticle size chips, mainly but
not     limited to the CoPoS packaging (meaning it can be used for large     CoWoS
packaging as well), although our checks indicate that glass     core substrate and

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
July 6, 2026 02:20 AM GMT

### Page 2

M
 Idea
2

From our foundry supply chain checks, CoPoS could be adopted in the Feynman Ultra in 
2029, while the first generation Feynman in 2028 would use TSMC’s 3D stack (die to 
wafer) for GPU stacking with SiC carriers to assist with chip thermal management.  The key 
question is whether TSMC will bring forward the CoPoS timeline for Feynman in 2028.
According to Derrick Yang (Greater China – Displays: Progress for Glass in Advanced 
Packaging among Panel Makers [29 Jun 2026]), we do see the potential of glass panels in 
semiconductors, as the package size gets bigger – though a more realistic timing for HPC 
adoption looks to be 2028/2029. Innolux will conduct the TGVs (Through Glass Vias) 
process on a glass panel (510mm*515mm), before sending that to Ibiden for substrate 
processes. For Innolux, the challenges lie in forming TGVs and the subsequent copper 
plating, because this is not part of the regular display panel production. Our 
understanding is that Innolux will need to procure new equipment for glass cores. The 
major CoPoS fab will be TSMC AP7 (see Exhibit 5 ), located in Tainan, Taiwan, close to 
Innolux’s factory. Innolux‘s TGV production is likely to be in 2028, and we see a small 
chance of early adoption at Feynman. 
Howard Kao, who covers Greater China substrate names, indicated there could be more 
substrate vendors apart from Ibiden to supply the glass core substrates in future. We 
believe the major technology bottleneck is the TGV process given glass scrap issues; 
again, we think 2029 is more likely timing for CoPoS.
3. GlassBridge to replace or complement to FAU in CPO? Fiber Array Units (FAU) are 
used to connect the optical fibers to the optical engines around the networking chips. 
According to our hardware analyst Andy Meng’s report, Greater China Technology 
Hardware: GlassBridge and Fiber‑to‑PIC's Implications for AI Transceiver and FAU Makers 
(28 Jun 2026), Corning GlassBridge is a fiber to PIC connector platform that brings optical 
fiber directly to a photonic integrated circuit (PIC). For AI transceiver companies (e.g. 
Eoptolink, covered by Andy), the impact should be limited as GlassBridge can be used in 
both CPO and NPO. However, GlassBridge adoption timing remains to be seen, along with 
whether it would complement FAU based approaches by offering a wafer based, passively 
aligned alternative. 
According to  our semiconductor analyst, Tiffany Yeh (see her latest report, link), we do 
not rule out that GlassBridge could hamper FAU's TAM in the long run, but we are yet to 
see any projects in TSMC's COUPE platform that have adopted this technology. On the 
other hand, GlassBridge mainly serves edge coupling, and can only currently support 
one‑dimension fiber layouts. However, TSMC's mainstream COUPE platform and key 
customers' solutions, such as NVIDIA, AMD (both covered by Joseph Moore), and Ayar 
Labs, are still sticking to grating coupling for the next few years, as it is easier to achieve 
mass production – which we expect in 2H26. Lastly, CPO requires integrated design and 
discussion across the whole ecosystem – including foundry (OE), chip designer, FAU, laser, 
and even system integrator – so it is hard to change a design overnight. GlassBridge is an 
ideal solution but we believe it remains far from mass production. We see TSMC scaling 
PIC capacity to 25kwpm in 2028, with Ins2 test efficiency improving. We stay overweight 
on CPO enablers, as investor expectations are already on the floor.

Morgan Stanley Research 5
Overview of Technologies discussed in this Report
CoPoS
Chip-on-Panel-on-Substrate (CoPoS) is an emerging TSMC technology aimed at scaling
package size and reducing cost: By moving toward panel-level or large-area RDL-based
packaging, CoPoS enables much larger integration footprints beyond the reticle limits of
silicon interposers. This makes it attractive for next-generation AI systems that require
integrating many large dies. Its advantages lie in scalability and potential cost efficiency at

large sizes, but it is less mature than CoWoS and offers lower interconnect density. As
such, it is not yet ideal for the most bandwidth-critical interfaces, and is still evolving in
terms of ecosystem and manufacturability.
However, given the recent needs of larger sizes of chip design, and competition from Intel
(covered by Joseph Moore), we believe the schedule for TSMC CoPoS could be pulled
forward earlier to 2028, in the hope of meeting the needs of those GPU or AI ASICs

adopting 2nm process or below.

Glass Core Substrates
Though the ultimate goal of glass-core substrates is to support multiple high-performance
computing chips, they can also serve applications with less demanding specifications.
Therefore, before glass sees adoption as an interposer in advanced packaging, we believe
it could gain traction as a substrate core, replacing the conventional resin and glass-cloth
composite core used in select high-end organic substrate applications. This should not be

interpreted as glass cloth becoming obsolete. We expect glass-core adoption to be
gradual, while mainstream ABF and other organic substrates should continue to rely on
glass-cloth-reinforced resin cores in the near term.
Glass has several advantages over  organic cores, including:
• Fine line width/space and via pitch: Down to 2um in line width/space, 75um on via
pitch can accommodate more I/Os for more chips with more complex functions
(50% more dies on a chip) or reduce the metal layers

• Higher bump density: Die-to-die bump pitch is less than 35um on substrate to
reduce die area and power consumption
• High speed I/O: Smooth copper interconnects, ultra low-loss dielectric and fine
TGV pitch together can enable high speed transmission at lower cost vs. optical
solutions
• Better mechanical strength: Less warpage and distortion amid  elevated
temperatures during the packaging process
• Similar CTE as chips: Less mismatch of coefficient of thermal expansion (CTE) can

mitigate the warpage issue

Glassbridge/FAU
On June 24, Corning (covered by Meta Marshall) officially launched its GlassBridge glass
optical interconnect assembly at the AI Data Center Optical Communications and

Interconnect Technology Conference held in Seoul. Based on Corning's document:
1) Corning GlassBridge is a fiber‑to‑PIC connector platform that brings optical fiber
directly to a photonic integrated circuit (PIC).

### Page 8

M
Idea
8
2) Fiber‑to‑PIC technology refers to the optical interface that couples optical fiber
directly into a PIC rather than through a long fiber assembly.
3) Traditional Fiber Array Units (FAU) become complex to assemble and scale at very high
fiber counts. GlassBridge complements FAU‑based approaches by offering a wafer‑based,
passively aligned alternative that supports higher density, improved scalability, and
detachable system integration.
In CPO development, FAU makers are likely to face disruption risk with GlassBridge

emerging as a new technology approach. For AI transceiver companies, the impact should
be limited as GlassBridge can be used in both CPO and NPO. Wider application in NPO
could potentially offset CPO risk.

### Page 1

2026 年 7 月 6 日 • 產業概況更新

伺服器產業

ASIC 數量仍在增加中，GB300 受 xAI 需求出貨量提升

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。

蘇子錚 
Alex.Su@yuanta.com 
劉東萌 
miketmliu@yuanta.com

元大觀點 
⧫  
xAI 上修其 2H26 GB300 訂單需求，預期訂單將上修 1.3 萬櫃，有利於
組裝廠 SMCI 與緯創相關供應鏈，對於水冷、機構件廠亦為正向。 
⧫  
2027 年 AWS T3 水冷訂單上修，預計數量將達 2-2.5 萬櫃，且明年將
新增新推論機櫃，緯穎、奇鋐、勤誠將受惠。 
⧫  
ASIC 將成為明年最大成長動能，均熱片用量、鍍金需求、水冷 滲透率
提升，相關受惠業者包含：奇鋐、富世達、健策、邁科、匯鑽科等。 
GB300 受惠 xAI 增加，下半年出貨大增 
根據供應鏈調查，xAI 近期有對兩家組裝廠上修 GB300 訂單約 1.3 萬櫃，出
貨時程將會落在 3Q26-1Q27，其中 Supermicro 得到的訂單較為明顯，該
GB300 採用 in Rack CDU 較標準品 GB300 略不同，需要搭配風冷背門運作
(Fan Door or RDHx)。我們上修 2026 年 GB300 出貨量 24%至 6.8 萬櫃，
並預期 VR200 出貨量為 2,000 櫃，本次主要受惠廠商包括 Supermicro 及緯
創的相關供應鏈，對於水冷、機殼零組件也有利，如緯創、高力、雙鴻、奇
鋐等。 
AWS T3 水冷訂單上修，新推論機櫃明年量產  
根據調查，AWS 近期上修 2027 年 AWS T3 水冷機櫃的需求，幅度約 1 倍，
預估明年的出貨櫃數將會達到 2-2.5 萬櫃。原先受到資料中心建置速度不如
預期，造成水冷機櫃展望低於預期，目前 見其進度皆有所好轉。 本中心原先
預期 2027 年 AWS ASIC 僅 280 萬顆，觀察到其 CoWoS 上調後，預期出貨
量將達到 340 萬顆、上修 21%，並預期水冷機櫃 2027 年至少出貨量達 2 萬
櫃，且 AWS 希望水冷比重能再提升，後續仍有上修可能。另外，本中心觀
察到，AWS 2027 年將會有新推論型 ASIC 專案，預計名稱為 EA100 或
T3.5，預計 1H27 開始量產。相關個股如緯穎、勤誠、奇鋐、富世達、邁科
將受惠。 
ASIC 為 2027 年最大動能，AWS、Google 向前衝 
我們預估 2027 年 ASIC 總晶片量將達到 1,395 萬個、年增 108%，主要動能
來自 Google 及 AWS，其中又以 Google 成長幅度最高，在 ASIC 需求提升
下，不論是均熱片用量提升、鍍金需求、水冷 滲透率皆有所增加。本中心認
為對於相關供應鏈如 緯穎、勤誠、奇鋐、富世達、邁科 、健策、匯鑽科的
2027 年業績皆有正向幫助。

產業分析  
受惠於 xAI 強勁需求，預估 2H26 GB300 出貨量明顯成長 
根據供應鏈調查，xAI 近期有對兩家組裝廠上修 GB300 訂單約 1.3 萬櫃，出貨時程將會落在 3Q26-
1Q27，其中 Supermicro 得到的訂單較為明顯，該 GB300 採用 in Rack CDU 較標準品 GB300 略
不同，需要搭配風冷背門運作(Fan Door or RDHx) 。我們上修 2026 年 GB300 出貨量 24%至 6.8
萬櫃，並預期 VR200 出貨量為 2,000 櫃，這次主要受惠的供應商為 Supermicro 及緯創的相關供應
鏈。 
VR200 的部分，受到晶片封裝段的些微調整，出貨時程略為遞延，原先小量產時程落在 8-9 月，但
目前有調整至 9-10 月，約落延後 1 個月，影響有限，我們預估 2026 年的 VR 出貨量為 2,000 櫃；
另外，我們觀察到和碩有取得來自 xAI 的 VR200 訂單，預計 2Q27 開始出貨，出貨量可望達上千
櫃。

資料來源：元大投顧預估

ASIC 總量在提升，AWS 需求強勁 
根據調查，AWS 近期上修 2027 年 AWS T3 水冷機櫃的需求，幅度約 1 倍，預估 2027 年的出貨櫃
數將會達到 2-2.5 萬櫃。原先受到資料中心建置速度不如預期，造成水冷機櫃展望低於預期，目前
看來進度皆有所好轉。我們原先預期 2027 年 AWS ASIC 僅 280 萬顆，觀察到其 CoWoS 上調後，
預期出貨量將達到 340 萬顆、上修 21%，並預期水冷機櫃 2027 年至少出貨量為 2 萬櫃，且 AWS
希望水冷比重能再提升，後續仍有上修可能 ，相關個股如緯穎、勤誠、奇鋐、富世達、邁科將受惠。  
另外，本中心觀察到，AWS 2027 年將會有新推論型 ASIC 專案，預計名稱為 EA100 或 T3.5，預
計一櫃將會有 128 個晶片，且單價可望優於水冷架構的 T3 機櫃，預計 2Q27 開始量產。

### Page 3

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 7 月 6 日  第3頁，共5頁   
圖 2：ASIC 出貨量預估

資料來源：元大投顧預估

TPU 鍍金水冷板預計 8 月開始小量生產，並逐步放量 
Google 的 TPU v8 晶片預計將再 TIM 2 改以混合金屬介質(石墨/銦複合材料，VInGr)，由於採用銦
材質，水冷板在接觸後會產能 金屬間化合物(氧化)，導致接觸面脆化，因此水冷板 下方的金屬面需
進行鍍金加工，確保銦與接觸面粘著，維持長期熱傳穩定。 
鍍金的價格取決得產品不同，TPU v8 的鍍金價格約 30 美元，且因鍍金會產生劇毒加上技術門檻較
高，電鍍牌照申請時間較長，因此部分業者如奇鋐、鴻海需要專門的電鍍代工廠，目前 Google 
TPU 由台廠及大廠電鍍為主，且因業者對於鍍金厚薄度的要求相當嚴苛，代工廠需有三軸檢測儀的
檢測技術，因此代工廠商相對較少，過往有相關量產實績如匯鑽科。 
另外，根據調查，因 TPU v8 4Q26 將開始明顯放量，因此鍍金廠將於 7 月開始生產，8 月開始小
量出貨，預計 9 月開始單月出貨量有望提升至 10 萬片，並逐月往上提升；除了上述的鍍金產品外，
匯鑽科也有鍍鎳產品用於光收發模組外殼及探針卡等產品。我們認為 ，TPU 2026 年出貨量為 380
萬個，2027 年在 TPU V8 的帶動下將年增 111%達到 800 萬個，為相關供應鏈帶來明顯成長動能。

圖 4：Google ASIC 出貨量預估

資料來源：元大投顧預估

可卸式均熱片，Rubin Ultra 散熱方案之一 
根據供應鏈調查，NVIDIA 正在研發一種新版本的水冷板，其功能類似 MCL 可以讓水冷板直接與主
晶片接觸並帶走熱源。此方案的要點在於採用可拆卸式上蓋 (Removable Lid) 設計，主晶片與均熱
片封裝後運至 L6 系統組裝廠(如鴻海、緯創等)時，將移除部分原有的上蓋 (Lid)及中間的導熱介面
材料(TIM2)，隨後將客製化的水冷板直接封裝上去。特別的地方是，該水冷板底部異於傳統的平面
設計，而是呈現曲面或凸起形狀，以確保與主晶片表面達到最緊密的接觸。  
目前該方案還在測試階段，主要差別在於可拆部分需使用螺絲鎖，或採用磁吸式做為可拆除的一部
分。我們預期 4Q26 有望能確定是否採用 Removable Lid，並用於 Rubin Ultra，由於介質層減少至
一層，可提高水冷板的散熱上限，單價部分會再次提升，有利於健策營運 。 
圖 5：Removeable Lid 可望在 Rubin Ultra 中採用

Summary: Auras reported its June 2026 revenue after the market close today (7 July 
2026) of TWD2,718m (-13.4% MoM and +62.1% YoY; +77.3% YoY for 6M26). Its 2Q26 
revenue accounts for 99.5% and 99.0% of our and the consensus forecasts of 2Q26 
revenue, respectively.

We have a Buy (1) rating and 12-month TP of TWD1,670, based on a target PER of 25x 
applied to our one-year-forward EPS forecast. For more information on the company, 
please refer to our latest flash, 1Q26 review: results beat market consensus, published on 
6 May 2026.

What’s the impact 
• 2Q26 revenue came in line with our and the Bloomberg consensus forecasts. On 7 
July 2026, Auras reported its monthly revenue in June 2026 of TWD2,718m (-13.4% 
MoM and +62.1% YoY; +77.3% YoY for 6M26). We attribute the MoM decline to delayed 
shipments in June. Its 2Q26 revenue of TWD8,699m (+1.7% QoQ and +63.9% YoY) 
accounted for 99.5% and 99.0%, respectively, of our (TWD8,742m, +2.2% QoQ and 
+64.8% YoY) and the Bloomberg consensus forecasts for 2Q26 revenue. For 2Q26 
product mix by revenue: server 78% (+184% YoY), PC 17% (-10% YoY), VGA 4% (-69% 
YoY) and others 1% (-45% YoY). Liquid cooling revenue accounted for 55% of its 2Q26 
revenue (same as 1Q26).

AURAS: consolidated monthly sales

Source: Company

• 3Q26 guidance. In 3Q26, demand for GB300 is likely to be better than in 2Q26, 
therefore the company expects its revenue for 3Q26 to grow QoQ.

What we recommend 
We have a Buy (1) rating and 12-month TP of TWD1,670, based on a target PER of 25x 
applied to our one-year-forward EPS forecast. Based on our 2026/27E EPS, the stock is 
currently trading at PERs of 17.4x/12.8x, vs. its past-3-year trading range of 11-38x. Key 
downside risks: softer-than-expected end-market demand, penetration rate of new specs 
and liquid cooling developments.

Document 105: 260707_ML_kyber-delay

Source PDF: all report pdf/260707_ML_kyber-delay.pdf

Date: 2026-07-07

### Page 1

Technology - Taiwan

Thoughts on Rubin Ultra spec delay; higher 
confidence on testing interface/substrate

Industry Overview

Share price likely hit by delay/cancellation of certain specs 
Share prices were weak (down 8-9% on average) on July 6th across the subsectors of 
CCL/PCB/substrate and testing interface within our coverage, due likely to the concerns 
surrounding the delay (or even cancellation) of the Kyber rack and CPO adoption from 
Rubin Ultra to Feynman platform, as well as the adoption of 2-compute die (rather than 
4-compute die) structure of GPU. Although such concerns are valid from the technology 
perspective considering the constraint from CCL material, warpage issue of big-sized 
substrates, and the reticle size in AP, we see the share price correction as an enhanced 
entry point for Buy-rated EMC, TUC, Unimicron, Kinsus, NYPCB, MPI and WinWay in the 
groupd, as we consider the AI server capex to remain in the upcycle toward 2028.  
Demand scale-down amidst ongoing supply constraint 
On CCL/PCB/substrate, we see the potential delay/cancellation as a demand scale-down 
in response to the supply constraint, which is analogous to the concern we saw in (see 
report) DRAM for Vera CPU. Yet, we flag a likely steadier demand growth for 
CCL/PCB/substrate vs other parts (e.g. memory) used in AI server system given their 
lower price elasticity, grounded by a smaller mix (LSD/MSD %) out of total BoM costs. 
Further, we believe (see reports) high-end CCL (M8-grade & above) and ABF substrates 
will remain in under-supply until at least end-2027, given the lengthy equipment lead-
time vs more structural demand growth driven by server CPU/accelerators.  
Still expect Q-based M9 to penetrate faster vs PTFE  
Although both quartz-based and PTFE-based CCL are considered to enhance the Dk and 
Df, the production difficulty of each solution is located on PCB and CCL side 
respectively. Such difference makes us expect a higher chance for Q-glass based M9-
grade CCL to penetrate faster in AI server system, along with the improving yield rate on 
PCB side. In near/mid-term, we also flag demand upside (or wider S/D gap) for high-end 
CCL, if the improvement of PCB yield rate (based on M9-Q glass) remains mild.  
Pecking order: testing interface/substrate, CCL then PCB 
In our view, the subsectors being more immune to the inflection of AI server upcycle will 
be testing interface/substrate, CCL, PCB by pecking order, as the former ones are closer 
to the chip-level solution with spec advancement. For CCL, we flag TUC’s bigger upside 
in m/s and pricing in N-T/M-T, vs EMC’s chance from substrate material and scarcity 
based on technology advantage (M9-Q) in L-T. For substrate and testing interface, the 
content downside (from 2-die solution) could be mitigated by unit upside. We also see 
the worry over substrate price cuts (by memory customers) is unwarranted given the 
shrinking BT substrate supply, elevated costs of glass cloth vs strong memory demand.

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Price objective basis & risk 
Elite Material (ETMCF) 
Our PO of NT$7,800 is based on 34x 2H27-1H28E P/E, at the high-end of its historical 
range of 6-34x since 2016. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, given the market-
share expansion in server/networking, where we see structural growth in addressable 
market.

Upside risks are (1) share gain at substrate CCL, (2) stronger 5G smartphone shipment, 
(3) greater 5G smartphone spec upgrades, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
change.

Downside risks are (1) share loss at iPhone, (2) keener competition in high-end CCL, and 
(3) unfavorable FX and raw material cost change. 
Kinsus (KNSUF) 
We set our PO at NT$1,070 based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at mid-to-high-end 
level in its historical trading range (8-40x) since 2013. In our view, the multiple is backed 
by ROE and operating margin recovery to 30% and 23% in 2027-28E.

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) increasing adoption of SiP and AiP, 
(3) lower competition pressure and (4) slow development of alternative technology.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, (3) 
more severe competition, and (4) unfavorable technology development. 
MPI Corporation (XMJCF) 
Our PO NT$8,250 is based on 40x 2028E P/E, which is at the high-end of the company's 
historical trading range (10x-40x). In our view, the valuation multiple is underpinned by 
1) 77% earnings CAGR in 2026-28E, 2) operating margin of 43-44% in 2027-28E, 3) 
ROE of 55-56% in 2027-28E, 4) net cash of NT$33/share, and 5) free cash flow 
generation of NT$180/share in 2027-28E.

Downside risks to our PO are: 
1) slower end market demand, which may undermine the company's revenue growth and 
operating leverage 
2) customers' switch from VPC probe card to MEMS solution, where MPI has a less 
dominant position 
3) keener competition especially from its foreign competitors, which may dampen the 
margin profile 
4) failure to execute or delay of in-sourcing strategy on PCB, which may limit the firm's 
delivery/supply to customers.

### Page 3

NYPCB (NANYF) 
Our PO of NT$1,430 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which in our view, the multiple 
backed by ROE and operating margin expansion to 37% and 30% in 2027-28E, as well as 
a net cash (to equity) position of 21%.

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) higher SiP/AiP adoption, and (3) 
less intensified competition pressure in PCB business.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, 
and (3) more severe competition in PCB business. 
Taiwan Union Technology Corporation (TWUNF) 
Our PO NT$2,600 is based on 30.5x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at the high-end of the 
historical trading range of 8-31x during 2016-2026. In our view, the valuation multiple is 
justified, given 1) gross/operating margin expansion to 29% and 22-23% in 2026-28E, 
2) expanding ROE toward around 53% in 2026-28E, and 3) robust free-cash flow 
generation in 2027-28E.

Downside risks are 
1) Fluctuation in raw material prices, which could lead to a more volatile gross margin 
for TUC in the near term 
2) More intensified competition in CCL, which could pressure TUC's overall margin 
profile 
3) Adoption of new type of upstream material (e.g., quartz), which could bring business 
uncertainty for the names in CCL industry, including TUC 
4) Worse-than-expected execution by the company, which could cause market-share loss 
and/or decreased business traction with customers 
Unimicron (UMCRF) 
Our PO of NT$1,400 is based on 33x 2H27-1H28E, which is roughly 1x standard 
deviation above the historical average of 20x since 2017. In our view, the multiple is 
appropriate as we expect Unimicron earnings to be supported by its high-end technology 
offerings, where end market demand seems more resilient.

Upside risks are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) greater PC and regular server demand, and (3) 
lower competition pressure.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) lower ASP content increase 
at AI server, and (3) bigger drop in the demand of consumer electronic products. 
WinWay Technology (XWCLF) 
Our PO NT$11,800 is based on 37x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at mid-to-high-end of the 
company's historical trading range (9-54x). In our view, such valuation multiple can be 
supported by 1) operating margin expansion to 40%, 2) ROE expansion to around 71%, 
3) free cash flow generation of NT$316/share in 2027-28, 4) net cash/share of around 
NT$31, and 5) its meaningful revenue exposure (higher than 50%) to AI/HPC 
applications, where the growth is more structural.

Downside risks are: 
1) slower capex growth by datacenter/hyperscale cloud service providers (CSPs), which 
will undermine the demand of AI GPU/ASIC and correspondingly pose a negative impact 
on the consumption of WinWay's testing socket 
2) less satisfactory execution in the capacity expansion (especially when the pins used in 
testing socket remain under-supply), which could be resulted from energy shortage 
amidst the geopolitical tensions and could interrupt the delivery by WinWay and 
undermine its track record to the customers 
3) a shortfall in customers' demand or share loss in key customers, which could bring

### Page 4

significant impact to WinWay given its concentrated revenue exposure to top 3 
customers 
4) a business model change in probe card business which makes WinWay being 
bypassed, especially when the company still lacks the key technology know-how in pins 
(used in probe card).

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 -  Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment rating 
of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, the 0-
12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a stock 
should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst ’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

UBS Global Data Central 2.0
Welcome to our second edition of the UBS Global Data Centre Central – our new, one-
stop shop product bringing together our key research, insights and newsflow on the
Data Centre / AI theme (DCC 1.0).
What is key in today’s piece?
 We build out further our work on 800VDC power architecture transition with an

introduction of an 800VDC Primer and a dedicated Expert Call (23 July at 2pm
UKT, register here)
 Raise our forecasts for Data Centre Equipment market growth
 Build out further valuation and positioning tracking across the AI/Data
Centre ecosystem
 Offer our views on latest newsflow including Meta's push to sell excess AI
compute capacity, Google's liquid-to-air cooling sidecar, and France's data centre
build-out progress.
Raising market outlook for DC Equipment space

  We now forecast the Data Centre Equipment market (€m/revenue terms) to grow
25% in 2026, 20% in 2027 and 10-15% in 2028-2030 vs previously 20-25% in
2026, 15-20% in 2027 and 10-15% in 2028-30. The key drivers for the upgrade
are 1/ Strong Q1 orders performance across the key players and consistently
positive commentary on Q2 developments and 2/ Latest UBS Evidence Lab data
pointing to a substantial increase in under construction and planned capacity

pipeline versus beginning of the year. We clearly see further potential upside to our
new forecasts but we are slightly held back by the UBS Evidence Lab showing
flattening of yoy growth in MW additions to total global capacity in Q1 this year
(strong growth in US vs a substantial slowdown in Asia Pac) and hence we look for
further data points from Q2 reporting and subsequent data releases.
 Stock calls
 In our European Capital Goods coverage , our top picks in this space are

Siemens, Schneider and Munters ( all Buy rated) while we are Neutral on ABB
and Legrand and have a Sell on Belimo.
 With respect to our US Industrials coverage , we remain positive on all
industrial companies exposed to AI infrastructure investment, with our
preference reflecting quality of execution on the expected growth. We expect
content per MW to increase across the board as more power delivery is
required to the rack. GE Vernova, Vertiv and Eaton appear best positioned to us

with respect to 800VDC dynamics. GEV solid state transformers are being co-
funded by a hyperscaler customer, and we expect testing to start in September
followed by commercialization about 6 months after that. In Machinery, we
remain positive on Cummins. In E&C, we continue to expect PWR, MTZ, PRIM,
and DY to benefit from a tight labor supply market in fiber and electrical work
on data centers.
 In Asia, we continue to see Delta Electronics well positioned over the next few

We now forecast the Data Centre Equipment market (€m/revenue terms) to grow 25%
in 2026, 20% in 2027 and 10-15% in 2028-2030 vs previously 20-25% in 2026, 15-

20% in 2027 and 10-15% in 2028-30. The key drivers for the upgrade are 1/ Strong Q1
orders performance across the key players and consistently positive commentary on Q2
developments and 2/ Latest UBS Evidence Lab data pointing to a substantial increase in
under construction and planned capacity pipeline versus beginning of the year. We
clearly see further potential upside to our new forecasts but we are slightly held back by
the UBS Evidence Lab showing flattening of yoy growth in MW additions to total global

capacity in Q1 this year (strong growth in US vs a substantial slowdown in Asia Pac) and
hence we look for further data points from Q2 reporting and subsequent data releases.
UBS Evidence Lab Global Data Centre Monitor
According to UBS Evidence Lab data, global operational capacity increased by 28GW in
2025 from 90GW in 2024, reaching a total of 118GW. This is higher than implied from
the 21GW capacity under construction at the end of 2024. North America/APAC/Europe

accounts for 45%/40%/14% of the total capacity, respectively.
Planned capacity increased significantly y/y, by 66GW or 88%, reaching 141GW by the
end of 2025. North America continues to dominate, accounting for 65% of the planned
capacity  while APAC and Europe are both at 16-17%. Under construction capacity is at
34GW globally by end of 2025. We see this as a good indicator of the capacity addition
within a 1-year time frame, and see upside from this based on the evidence from

2024/25.  In Q1 2026, operational capacity increased by another 6GW while planned
capacity saw a significant growth of 20GW.

Hyperscaler capex
We continue to see sustained capex intensity among hyperscalers, with aggregate capex

capex.

 Expert call with Dell'Oro Group (link). 1/ Data center capacity additions to
remain strong: Capacity additions are expected to scale from >25GW in 2025 to

 Expert call on 800VDC  ( link). 800V DC architecture is emerging as the next
standard in data centres, driven by rising rack density, with adoption still early but

expected to accelerate. It delivers c.1–3% efficiency gains per conversion, with
potential total power savings of ~5%, alongside lower system costs, reduced
copper usage and simplified infrastructure (less need for UPS, inverters and PDUs).
While insulation and EMI challenges remain, the shift will favour high-voltage
technology players and reduce demand for traditional low-voltage components.

### Page 6

Data Centre Central UBS Research
800VDC Architecture 101
800V DC is emerging as a structural shift in data centre power architecture, driven by
rapidly rising rack densities that are pushing traditional low-voltage AC systems to their
limits. By increasing voltage, operators can reduce current for a given power level, which
lowers copper usage, reduces heat losses and improves efficiency—effectively enabling
continued scaling toward several hundred kW and ultimately MW-class racks.

Architecturally, this implies a move from distributing low-voltage AC across the facility to
rectifying power earlier and distributing high-voltage DC, with conversion happening
closer to the chip. This reduces the role of low-voltage AC switchgear and PDUs, while
increasing the importance of DC distribution, protection and power electronics.
Medium-voltage infrastructure remains in place, and UPS evolves rather than
disappears, shifting toward lithium-ion and more distributed configurations.

Adoption is expected to be phased. Near-term solutions (e.g. sidecar or centralized DC
architectures) focus on supporting higher rack density while leveraging existing supply
chains. Over time, the industry is likely to move toward more fully DC-based systems
incorporating solid-state transformers and advanced DC-DC conversion. However,
multiple architectures are likely to coexist for an extended period, suggesting a gradual
transition rather than a rapid, standardised shift. The efficiency benefits are meaningful

but incremental, with savings driven by fewer conversion steps and lower copper
intensity, partially offset by greater spend on DC switchgear, controls and protection.
The transition also introduces new technical challenges, including insulation, safety and
EMI management, increasing system complexity.
From a vendor perspective, the shift primarily drives a mix change. Exposure to high-
voltage systems, DC distribution and advanced power electronics becomes increasingly

valuable, favouring players with strong capabilities in these areas. Conversely, pure low-
voltage AC exposure faces structural pressure unless offerings are expanded up the
voltage stack. Overall, 800V DC represents an evolution of the electrical architecture,
redistributing value toward higher-voltage, more integrated and more software-enabled
solutions rather than reducing total addressable spend.
Figure 10: Data Centre architecture available today

Source: Company reports, UBS estimates.

### Page 7

Figure 11: Three phases of 800V DC architecture
Source: Company reports, UBS estimates. *Icon represents 800V DC sidecar power distribution unit.
Today's data centre power architecture is fundamentally an AC distribution system.
Power enters the facility at medium voltage, is stepped down through transformers to LV
AC, passes through UPS systems, and is distributed around the data hall via AC busbars
before finally reaching rack PDUs. Conversion to the DC voltages required by chips

ultimately occurs inside server power supplies.
The architecture is well proven and highly resilient, but it requires multiple conversion
stages and significant quantities of LV electrical equipment. As rack densities increase
beyond traditional enterprise and cloud workloads, the efficiency and physical footprint
of this power chain become increasingly important design constraints
Figure 12: Current data centre architecture (version 1)
Source: UBS

### Page 8

A second common architecture inserts additional distribution layers, including central
PDUs and remote power panels, between the UPS and IT equipment. This approach
improves operational flexibility and allows operators to support larger deployments, but
adds further equipment, floor space and electrical conversion stages.
Figure 13: Current data centre architecture (version 2)
Source: UBS
Phase 1 represents the first meaningful shift away from traditional AC distribution.

While the upstream architecture remains largely intact, an AC-to-DC power shelf is
introduced before the rack line-up, creating an 800V DC distribution bus that feeds the
racks directly.
The key change is that power conversion moves closer to the facility level rather than
occurring repeatedly inside individual servers. Rack-level equipment receives 800V DC
and converts it to 48V or 12V DC for IT hardware. This reduces conversion losses,
simplifies server power architectures and helps support rack densities of c.150kW,

materially above most current deployments.
From a supplier perspective, the value pool begins to migrate away from conventional
rack PDUs towards high-power rectifiers, DC conversion systems and rack-level DC-DC
power electronics. However, we see the disruption to incumbent electrical vendors as
relatively limited given it does not remove a significant part of the existing installed base.
This makes ORV3 an attractive near-term migration path for operators seeking higher

rack densities without redesigning the entire facility power chain.

### Page 9

Figure 14: Phase 1 (ORV3) - introduction of 800V DC
Source: UBS
Figure 15: Evolution of current architecture to Phase 1
Source: UBS
Phase 2 introduces the sidecar architecture which pushes power conversion even closer
to the rack. Rather than distributing AC through busbars and multiple downstream
distribution devices, a dedicated sidecar power cabinet converts 400V AC directly into
Phase 1 adds an 800V DC layer to the
existing architecture, centralising

power conversion and enabling
higher rack densities with minimal
disruption to existing infrastructure

### Page 10

800V DC adjacent to the rack row.
Centralising conversion within the sidecar reduces the amount of power equipment
located within the rack itself, freeing up valuable compute space. The architecture is
designed to support rack densities approaching 400kW.
The sidecar model removes a larger portion of the traditional AC distribution stack.
Functions previously performed by busbars, rack PDUs and associated rack-level AC
infrastructure are consolidated into a dedicated sidecar conversion cabinet positioned

adjacent to the compute racks.
For electrical equipment suppliers, this phase represents a more meaningful architectural
transition than ORV3 because existing product categories begin to be substituted.
Figure 16: Phase 2 (sidecar architecture)
Source: UBS

### Page 11

Figure 17: Evolution of current architecture to sidecar
Source: UBS
Phase 3 removes the largest proportion of today's electrical infrastructure. Conventional
transformers, low-voltage switchboards, low-voltage UPS systems and much of the AC
distribution chain are replaced by medium-voltage UPS systems, solid-state transformers
and native DC distribution.
In this architecture, power is converted from 11kV AC directly into 800V DC, eliminating
several intermediate stages. Short-duration energy storage stabilises the DC bus and

supports the highly dynamic power requirements of future AI clusters. The facility
evolves from an AC distribution network into a predominantly DC power system.
If commercialised at scale, a full solid-state architecture could support rack densities
approaching 1MW while materially reducing conversion losses, equipment count and
electrical footprint.
Phase 2 replaces busbars and rack-
level AC distribution with sidecar
cabinets that convert 400V AC to

800V DC beside the rack, enabling up
to c.400kW rack densities and a more
significant shift away from
traditional AC infrastructure

Key Newsflow and research
 Learnings from Q2 previews
 Legrand mentioned they have a sidecar prototype which we think they will fully
launch at the CMD in September. They continue to see a neutral to positive
impact for 80% of sales, with a negative impact for 20% of its sales. They
expect hybrid (with sidecar) to begin generating sales in 2027/8 with the
market growing to 10-20% of all new IT loads in 2030.
 Schneider is now seeing the price impact in P&L in Q2. The message for H1

Gross Margin remained 'flat to slightly down', which we now view as closer to
flat than down. Regarding Data Centres, we expect strong dynamics to
continue.
 ABB mentioned Data Centre demand continues to stand out. They already
have DC breaker based on power electronics and semiconductor and also
rectifier in its Motion business. ABB are also developing an SST product and
does not plan on doing the sidecar since it believes it sits in a transitory

category. Additionally, ABB believe the MV UPS will be a very important
product category as it moves UPS from smaller scale to a more system level
power supply.
 Siemens saw Electrification and Data Centre growing strongly. We believe that
Data Centre demand will remain at elevated levels for a few years. We forecast
30% organic order growth in Q3'26.
 Expert session on European data center market outlook ( link). The European
data centre market continues to benefit from strong demand, with AI expected to

become the primary growth driver as AI workloads increase to 3.5x 2025 levels by
2030 and account for 71% of total demand (vs. 54% today). However, power
availability rather than demand is increasingly the main constraint, particularly in
FLAP-D markets, supporting growth in secondary markets such as Iberia. Despite a
temporary slowdown in hyperscaler spending during 2025, activity has
reaccelerated in 2026. The sector is also attracting a broader investor base as data

centres evolve into a hybrid real estate-infrastructure asset class, with permitting,
grid access and delivery certainty remaining more important drivers of returns than
construction cost inflation.
 Meta  makes cloud push to sell excess AI compute power capacity
(link).Meta is considering either selling "raw" compute capacity to external
companies or access to various AI models that are hosted on Meta’s existing
infrastructure. UBS Internet Services analyst flag that CEO Mark Zuckerberg had

previously floated both of these options on various public calls and as such this is
not new news.  As this new line of business was not part of what Mr. Zuckerberg
had previously articulated as long term opportunities, some investors may be wary
of a pivot. They think that either of these developments will plug some of the
duration problem that has ailed the stock, as selling cloud capacity or model access
will theoretically yield higher near-term revenue versus waiting for Meta Business

Agents and Meta AI chatbots to scale.
 Google unveils new open-source liquid-to-air cooling sidecar for air-cooled
environments (link). Google has introduced “Brazos,” an open-source, rack-
mounted, closed-loop liquid-to-air cooling sidecar designed to enable incremental
deployment of high-density liquid cooling within existing air-cooled data centers.
The system captures heat at the component level using liquid cooling and
dissipates it into the hot aisle via integrated heat exchangers, offering liquid-

cooling performance with air-cooling operational simplicity. Brazos is modular
(three units per rack, ~60kW total load), compatible with standard OCP ORv3
racks, and supports deionized water or glycol mixtures while operating on 40–60V
DC via rack busbars. It incorporates safety and management features such as leak
detection, pressure relief, and remote monitoring, and is already commercially
ready via partners. By open-sourcing its design, Google aims to accelerate industry

adoption of scalable cooling solutions for AI-driven high-power workloads,
extending broader Open Compute Project efforts alongside similar sidecar
approaches from Microsoft, Meta, and AWS. Our take:   We note that 60kW is
clearly above traditional compute at 20-30kW but well below the "AI grade" of
100kW+. We don’t think this represents a threat to data centre cooling companies
and would see it as  a rather generic solution trying to make higher densities work

### Page 14

without a full liquid to chip setup.
 Data4 confirms €5bn plan for 700MW AI data center in northern France
(link). Data4 has confirmed plans to develop a €5bn, 700MW AI-focused data
center campus in Escaudain, northern France, marking its largest project to date
and one of Europe’s largest single-site digital infrastructure investments.  The
project benefits from strong government backing, fast-track grid access, and a
strategically attractive location within a key European connectivity corridor, and

forms part of France’s broader ambition to position itself as a leading AI
infrastructure hub. Our take: Our colleagues in Transportation/Construction team
also recently noted an acceleration in planned Data centre projects in Europe/
France - they see it as   a potential tailwind mid-term. Earlier in the year, our
Sustainability team wrote about France entering a phase of structural overcapacity
in low-carbon electricity. In their opinion, France has spare, clean and reliable

power at scale, and with more stable marginal costs than fossil-linked systems, at a
time when demand from AI, data centres, industry and broader electrification is
accelerating.
 Alderbuck Energy to deploy solid-state transformer at San Diego
Supercomputer Center (link). The company will deploy its Nexus Power Unit,
which is a medium-voltage solid-state transformer that consolidates multiple
pieces of conventional grid equipment into a single modular system. This, the

company claims, cuts project capital costs by 20-30 percent, reduces physical
footprint by a similar margin, shortens construction timelines by 30-50 percent,
and offers up to four percent higher energy efficiency. The project will assess a
12kV AC-to-800VDC configuration against conventional 480V AC systems,
generating operational data on powering high-density AI workloads. Prior to
deployment, the unit will undergo testing and simulation at UC San Diego’s

DERConnect facility.
 South Korea announces US$500bn chip investment on huge AI demand
(link). South Korea has announced a record KRW 800 trillion (US$518bn)
investment to expand its semiconductor industry, driven by surging demand from
AI data centres, with plans to build four new memory fabrication plants in the
southwest to establish a second major production hub outside Seoul. Backed by
major industry players including Samsung and SK Hynix, the initiative  represents

one of the largest corporate investment commitments in the country to date.

### Page 34

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
The engineering sector is cyclical by nature. Our estimates and the share prices are therefore
likely to be sensitive to changes in expectations for global or regional growth and industrial
activity. Exchange rate fluctuations contribute additional risk to the sector. We value the
stocks in our engineering coverage universe based on the average of cash flow (DCF, residual
income and DDM) and static (multiple-based) valuation methodologies.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Catalyst Watch on Hon Precision (7769.TW)
Direction: Upside
Duration: Within 90 Days (expires 27 Aug 2026)
Date Added: 28 May 2026
Catalyst: Thematic driven
We are constructive on Hon Precision’s earnings growth outlook driven by its solid engagement in global leading AI projects.
For 2Q earnings, our estimates are higher than consensus as we expect higher margins from a better sales mix and operating

leverage, which we believe could drive strong >100% YoY earnings growth. Also, though a long-term opportunity, we believe
co-packaged optics (CPO) engagement with key customers could also drive significant upside potential.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 4

Hon Precision (7769.TW)
08 July 2026 Citi Research
4
Hon Precision
Valuation
Our target price of NT$11,000 is based on a target PER multiple of 36x applied to the average of our 2027E/28E EPS estimates.
Our PER target of 36x is set at 1-std above its 1-year PER average, justified by its high share of the AI/HPC testing handler
market, which we estimate could support an earnings CAGR of 77% in 2025-28, higher than its 3-year CAGR at c40% in 2022-
25. Our estimated 77% earning CAGR is also higher than the +40% average (Bloomberg consensus) of its global testing

equipment peers. As its peers have been trading at  average  36x PER on 2027/28 consensus EPS in the past 1 year, we believe
Hon Precision deserves a further re-rating to our target PER of 36x.
Risks
Our quantitative risk rating system, which looks at historical share price volatility, rates Hon Precision High Risk. However, we
are not assigning a High Risk rating as we forecast an earnings CAGR of >75%, significantly stronger than the growth outlook

for its global peers. Also, we note its dominant position in AI/HPC handler market and expanding market TAM opportunity,
which support our constructive view over its growth outlook.
Key downside risks that could impede the shares from reaching our target price: 1) Global AI demand slowdown  driving weaker
capacity build for semiconductor equipment; 2) unexpected share losses at its major AI projects; 3) slower-than-expected
capacity ramp at its new facilities, or the company taking a long time to find/secure new sites; 4) slower-than-expected

development of its CPO business; 5) shorter-than-expected testing time leading to lower demand for its products; 6) weak
adoption of high-premium cold plate products; and 7) increasing competition from new entrants.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do

AMD’s 2027 CoWoS allocation remains at 240k for now, with
combined MI455/MI450 shipments at 1.5mn. TPU MP timing
skews toward 4Q. Blackwell chip "inventory" turned out to be a
supply chain buffer  and will be fully consumed by 2026. We

expect ~7mn Rubin chips and 90k NVL72 racks in 2027.
Further updates on our AMD CoWoS allocation: We published our preliminary
2027 CoWoS allocation last week and received many investor queries on AMD's
(covered by Joe Moore) CoWoS number. Some MI300 series and upcoming MI500
production will still run in 2027. However, the MI400 series will come in two
versions: (1) MI455: the standard version with 2 compute dies and 12 HBM4 12hi,
paired with the Helios rack (18 CPUs and 72 GPUs), with Microsoft, AWS, and Oracle

as key customers; (2) MI450: a Meta-customized, half-size chip paired with 1
compute die and 6 HBM4 12hi (9 CPUs and 36 GPUs). We still expect AMD's 2027
CoWoS at 240k, but do not rule out execution risk,  given its prior record of
trimming CoWoS bookings in 2026. 2027 chip shipments: MI455 1mn and MI450
500k. For CPU, Venice is AMD's first CPU adopting CoWoS, and we see CoW
production concentrated in OSATs—ASE/SPIL, Amkor, and Powertech. Total CPU

chip shipments could reach 5.7–6mn units in 2027 vs. 1mn in 2026.
Google TPU  progress update: Our industry checks suggest KYEC's 3Q26 revenue
could grow close to 10% Q/Q, below our initial 15% Q/Q expectation, mainly due to
slight Rubin and Sunfish delays and MediaTek's smartphone SoC order trim. We do
see Sunfish shipments largely in 4Q, with full-year volume at 960k units. With
Sunfish and Rubin pushed out, we believe more revenue will concentrate in 4Q26 or

1Q27 without order cuts. Many investors asked about Zebrafish delays; we still see
its 4Q26 volume ramp unchanged.
Comparing the Blackwell chip/rack shipment and Rubin chip/rack shipment:  We
provided a chip vs. rack shipment comparison for Blackwell in last June's AI tracker
.
We now include HGX (8-GPU per server) in our chip consumption model, and treat
9 HGX servers as equivalent to one NVL72 rack. We expect 5.4mn Blackwell units in
2026, and chip volume could meet Grace Blackwell NVL72 demand by 2H26. Our

latest checks also suggest close to 7mn Rubin and Rubin Ultra units in 2027, while
total Rubin NVL72 server racks could reach 90k in 2027 ( Exhibit 12  ). All Blackwell
chip "inventory" turned out to be a supply-chain buffer and will be fully consumed
by 2026, so we see no need to worry about inventory issues. We think Rubin will
follow a similar pattern.

Meta ASIC progress update: On the other hand, Meta cancelled its original 2nm ASIC

1H27, with potential CoWoS out by 2027 year-end or 1H28.
